\displaystyle\qquad+2\,e^{\lambda_{k}}\vec{H}_{k}\cdot e^{\lambda_{k}}\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k,i,j}\right)+c_{i,j,l}\left(-\left\langle\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{k,l},\nabla^{\perp}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k,i,j}\right)\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{k,l},\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k,i,j}\right)\right\rangle\right)\bigg)\,e_{i}\wedge e_{j}.

Since
\vec{R}_{k}\in\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
for all
p<\infty
, we deduce that for all
i,j,l\in\left\{1,\cdots,m\right\}

\displaystyle\langle\nabla c_{i,j,l},\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}_{k,l}\rangle\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k,i,j}\right)+\langle\nabla c_{i,j,l},\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{k,l}\rangle\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k,i,j}\right)\in\bigcap_{p<\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k})),

Indeed, since for some
c_{i,j,l}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle c_{i,j,l}(x)=\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}c_{i,j,l}^{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\Gamma_{\alpha,\beta}^{\gamma}(\vec{\Phi}_{k}(x)),\quad\text{},

we deduce that

\displaystyle|\nabla c_{i,j,l}|\leq C_{0}e^{\lambda_{k}},

so that
\langle\nabla c_{i,j,l},\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}_{k,l}\rangle\in\bigcap_{p<\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))\displaystyle
.
From
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\in C^{0}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|e^{\lambda_{k}}\vec{H}_{k}\cdot e^{\lambda_{k}}\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k,i,j}\right)\right|\leq C|z|^{d_{k}}\delta(|z|)\leq C|z|^{d_{k}-1}\,.

Since
\nabla\lambda_{k}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}
and
\nabla\vec{R}_{k}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}
, using the previous
\mathrm{L}^{p}
bound on
e^{\lambda_{k}}
(see (
3.26
)), we get that

\displaystyle\partial_{\overline{z}}F_{k}(\vec{R}_{k})\in\mathrm{L}^{q}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))\qquad\text{for all}\;\,q<\dfrac{2}{2-\varepsilon}.

Therefore, we have
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\in\mathrm{W}^{2,q}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
for all
q<\dfrac{2}{2-\varepsilon}
, and by Sobolev embedding, we get
\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\in\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
for all
p<\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon}
, which shows in particular that
\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\in\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
and
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\in C^{0}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
, which suffices to our purpose. Indeed, by the pointwise bounds (
3.89
) and (
3.111
), we deduce that

\displaystyle|\nabla\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|

\displaystyle=2|\partial_{z}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|\leq e^{\lambda_{k}}|\vec{L}_{k}|+2e^{\lambda_{k}}|\vec{H}_{k}|+2|F_{k}(\vec{R}_{k})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{|z|}+f_{k}(z)+C\delta(|z|)+Ce^{\lambda_{k}}|\vec{R}_{k}|\leq\frac{C}{|z|}+h_{k}(z),

(3.117)

where
h_{k}
is bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
for all
p<\dfrac{2}{1-\varepsilon}
.

Furthermore,
\vec{R}_{k}
and
S_{k}
solve the system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla_{z}\vec{R}_{k}&=i\left((-1)^{n+1}\star_{h}\left(\vec{n}_{k}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\nabla_{z}\vec{R}_{k}\right)+(\partial_{z}S_{k})\star_{h}\vec{n}_{k}\right)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,\alpha R_{k})\\
\partial_{z}S_{k}&=-i\langle\nabla_{z}\vec{R}_{k},\star_{h}\vec{n}_{k}\rangle\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,\alpha R_{k}).\end{aligned}\right.

(3.118)

Therefore, there exists a linear map
F_{k}:\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\nabla_{z}\vec{R}_{k}=\partial_{z}\vec{R}_{k}+F_{k}(\vec{R}_{k}),

where

\displaystyle|F_{k}(\vec{R}_{k})|\leq Ce^{\lambda_{k}}|\vec{R}_{k}|.

(3.119)

Thus, we can rewrite the system (
3.118
) as

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{z}\vec{R}_{k}&=i\left((-1)^{n+1}\star\left(\vec{n}_{k}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\partial_{z}\vec{R}_{k}\right)+(\partial_{z}S_{k})\star_{h}\vec{n}_{k}+i\,F_{k}(\vec{R}_{k})+(-1)^{n+1}\star_{h}\left(\vec{n}_{k}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}F_{k}(\vec{R}_{k})\right)\right)\\
\partial_{z}S_{k}&=-i\langle\partial_{z}\vec{R}_{k},\star_{h}\vec{n}_{k}\rangle-i\langle F_{k}(\vec{R}_{k}),\star_{h}\,\vec{n}_{k}\rangle\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.120)

Since
\vec{R}_{k}
and
S_{k}
are bounded in
\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,\alpha R_{k}))
, we deduce by the Sobolev embedding that for all
1<p<\infty
, there exists
C_{p}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,\alpha R_{k}))}+\left\|S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,\alpha R_{k}))}\leq C_{p}<\infty,\quad\text{for all $0<\alpha<\alpha_{0}$ and $k\geq N$.}

(3.121)

In order to make the notations easier to read, we prove the following lemma (see
[
4
]
). Denote:

\displaystyle{u}_{r}={\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int}_{\mkern-10.0mu\partial B(0,r)}u\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi r}\int_{\partial B(0,r)}u\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}.

Lemma 3.17
.

Let
k,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
u\in\mathrm{W}^{1,1}(B(0,1),\mathbb{C})
,
f\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{C})
,
\vec{v}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{k}\mathbb{C}^{m})
,
\vec{w}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}\cap\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{k}\mathbb{R}^{m})
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}u=-i\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+f\right).

(3.122)

Let
0<r<R<\infty
and set
\Omega=B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)
. Assume that
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v})\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(\Omega)
and that

\displaystyle|\nabla\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{v})(z)|\leq\frac{C_{0}}{|z|}\,,\quad\text{for all $r\leq|z|\leq R$}.

(3.123)

Then

\displaystyle\left(\int_{r}^{R}\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)_{\rho}\right|^{2}\rho\,d\rho\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2\pi}\binom{n}{k}C_{0}\left\|\nabla\vec{w}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|\vec{w}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.124)

Proof.

Rewriting the equation (
3.122
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\,\mathrm{Re}\,(u)=-i\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+f\right),

and recalling that
\partial_{z}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{x}-i\,\partial_{y}\right)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{x}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)&=&&2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(-i\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+f\right)\right)&&=2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+f\right)\\
\partial_{y}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)&=-&&2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(-i\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+f\right)\right)&&=2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+f\right).\end{aligned}\right.

Recalling that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{r}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)&=&&\cos(\theta)\,\partial_{x}u\,&&+&&\sin(\theta)\,\partial_{y}u\\
\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)&=-&&\sin(\theta)\,\partial_{x}u\,&&+&&\cos(\theta)\,\partial_{y}u,\end{aligned}

we get

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)

\displaystyle=2\,\cos(\theta)\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+f\right)+2\,\sin(\theta)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+f\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{|z|}\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\langle z\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle+z\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+zf(z)\right)\,.

(3.125)

Now, notice that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{|z|}\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\langle z\,\partial_{z}\vec{v},\vec{w}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\langle\left(\cos(\theta)+i\sin(\theta)\right)\left(\partial_{x}-i\,\partial_{y}\right)\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{Im}\,\left(i\,\langle\left(\cos(\theta)+i\sin(\theta)\right)\left(\partial_{x}-i\,\partial_{y}\right)\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{w}),\vec{w}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\sin(\theta)\partial_{x}\,\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{v})-\cos(\theta)\partial_{y}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle+\langle\cos(\theta)\partial_{x}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v})+\sin(\theta)\partial_{y}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{r}\langle\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle+\langle\partial_{r}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle.

Using (
3.4
), the same computation for
z\partial_{z}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\,\mathrm{Re}\,(u)=-\frac{1}{r}\langle\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)+\langle\partial_{r}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle+2\,\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\frac{z}{|z|}f(z)\right).

(3.126)

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}{\mathrm{Re}\,(u)}_{r}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\partial_{r}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)(r,\theta)d\theta=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left\langle\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\right\rangle d\theta+\frac{1}{2\pi r}\int_{\partial B(0,t)}\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{Im}\,(u)d\theta

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\langle\partial_{r}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\frac{z}{|z|}f(z)\right)d\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left\langle\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}-\vec{w}_{t}\right\rangle d\theta+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\langle\partial_{r}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle d\theta+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\frac{z}{|z|}f(z)\right)d\theta.

Notice that by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have for all
\varphi\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega,\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle\int_{r}^{R}\left|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\varphi\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}\right|^{2}\rho\,d\rho\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{r}^{R}\int_{0}^{2\pi}|\varphi|^{2}\rho\,d\rho d\theta=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\Omega}|\varphi|^{2}|dz|^{2}.

Therefore, by the Minkowski inequality and (
3.123
) we have

\displaystyle\left(\int_{r}^{R}\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)_{\rho}\right|^{2}\rho\,d\rho\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{w}-\vec{w}_{|z|}\right|^{2}\frac{|dz|^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\langle\partial_{r}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v}),\vec{w}\rangle|^{2}|dz|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\frac{z}{|z|}f(z)\right)\right|^{2}|dz|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\vec{w}-\vec{w}_{|z|}|^{2}\frac{|dz|^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|\vec{w}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

Now, by (
3.93
), we infer

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{w}-\vec{w}_{|z|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\leq\binom{n}{k}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\nabla\vec{w}|d\mathscr{H}^{1},\quad\text{ for all $z\in B(0,1)$}.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the co-area formula twice, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|\vec{w}-\vec{w}_{|z|}|^{2}\frac{|dz|^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\leq\binom{n}{k}^{2}\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}\left(\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\nabla\vec{w}|d\mathscr{H}^{1}\right)^{2}\frac{|dz|^{2}}{|z|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi\,\binom{n}{k}^{2}\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\nabla\vec{w}|^{2}\frac{|dz|^{2}}{|z|}=(2\pi)^{2}\binom{n}{k}^{2}\int_{r}^{R}\left(\int_{\partial B(0,t)}|\nabla\vec{w}|^{2}d\mathscr{H}^{1}\right)dt

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{2}\binom{n}{k}^{2}\int_{B_{R}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}|\nabla\vec{w}|^{2}|dz|^{2}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{r}^{R}\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(u)_{\rho}\right|^{2}\rho\,d\rho\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2\pi}\binom{n}{k}C_{0}\left\|\nabla\vec{w}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|\vec{w}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{v})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Applying Lemma
3.17
to
S_{k}
in the equation (
3.120
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\alpha_{0}^{-1}r_{k}}^{\alpha R_{k}}\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k,\rho})\right|^{2}\rho\,d\rho\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq n(n-1)\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}C_{3}(n,\Lambda)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\left\|\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}.

Applying Lemma
3.17
to each of the
\frac{m(m-1)}{2}
components of
\vec{R}_{k}
, we get by the Minkowski inequality that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\alpha_{0}^{-1}r_{k}}^{\alpha_{0}R_{k}}\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k,\rho})\right|^{2}\rho\,d\rho\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq{n(n-1)}\Gamma_{1}(n)C_{3}(n\Lambda)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}

\displaystyle+\frac{n(n-1)}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\left\|\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(S_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}+\left\|\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}\right)+{n(n-1)}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}.

Recall now the following generalization (see
[
33
]
) of
[
4
, Lemma VI.
2
]
proved in
[
25
]
(see also
[
26
]
).

Lemma 3.18
.

There exists a universal constant
R_{0}>0
with the following property.
Let
0<4r<R<R_{0}
,
\Omega=B(0,R)\setminus\overline{B}(0,r)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
a,b:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
\nabla a\in\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega)
and
\nabla b\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, and
\varphi:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a solution of

\displaystyle\Delta\varphi=\nabla a\cdot\nabla^{\perp}b\qquad\text{in}\;\ \Omega.

For
r\leq\rho\leq R
, define

\displaystyle{\varphi}_{r}={\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.71397pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.05457pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.4484pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.27722pt}}\!\int}_{\mkern-10.0mu\partial B_{\rho}(0)}\varphi\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi\rho}\int_{\partial B_{\rho}(0)}\varphi\,d\mathscr{H}^{1}.

Then
\nabla\varphi\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, and there exists a positive constant
C_{0}>0
independent of
0<4r<R
such that for all
\left(\dfrac{r}{R}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\alpha<\dfrac{1}{2}
it holds:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{\alpha R}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}r})}\leq C_{0}\left\|\nabla a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla b\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+C_{0}\left\|\nabla{\varphi}_{\rho}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+C_{0}\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega)}.

From (
3.121
) we know that

\displaystyle\left\|S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}+\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}\leq C.

Lemma
3.18
, the system (
3.120
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality imply that

\displaystyle\int_{2\alpha_{0}^{-1}r_{k}}^{\frac{\alpha_{0}R_{k}}{2}}\left(\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k,\rho})\right|+\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k,\rho})\right|\right)d\rho\leq C\int_{2\alpha_{0}^{-1}r_{k}}^{\frac{\alpha_{0}R_{k}}{2}}\delta(\rho)\left(\int_{\partial B(0,\rho)}\left(|\nabla S_{k}|+|\nabla\vec{R}_{k}|\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}\right)d\rho

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{2\alpha_{0}^{-1}r_{k}}^{\frac{\alpha_{0}R_{k}}{2}}\delta^{2}(\rho)\rho\,d\rho\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left\|S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}+\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}\right)\leq C.

Furthermore, by the inequality (
3.69
), there exists
r_{0}\in(\frac{\alpha_{0}R_{k}}{4},\frac{\alpha_{0}R_{k}}{2})
such that if
r=\frac{\alpha_{0}R_{k}}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B_{r_{0}}(0)}\left(|S_{k}|+|\vec{R}_{k}|\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sqrt{3\pi}}{\log(2)}\left(\left\|S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0))}+\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\pi^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}(2r)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}}{\log(2)}\left(\left\|S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0))}+\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0))}\right).

(3.127)

Therefore, it holds

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k,r}-\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k,r_{0}})\right|+\left|\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k,r}-\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k,r_{0}})\right|\leq C_{n}\int_{2\alpha_{0}^{-1}r_{k}}^{r_{0}}\left(\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k,\rho})\right|+\left|\frac{d}{d\rho}\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k,\rho})\right|\right)d\rho\leq C

and

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k,r})|+|\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k,r})|\leq C+\frac{C}{(\alpha_{0}R_{k})^{\frac{1}{p}}}\left(\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}+\left\|S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}\right).

In particular, we get that
\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})_{\rho},\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k})_{\rho}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}([4\alpha_{0}^{-1}R_{k},\frac{\alpha_{0}R_{k}}{4}])
.
We deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k}\right)(z)-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k}\right)_{|z|}\right|\leq n\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\nabla\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\vec{R}_{k}\right)|d\mathscr{H}^{1}\leq n\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|d\mathscr{H}^{1}

\displaystyle+2n\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}e^{\lambda_{k}}\left(|\vec{L}_{k}|+|\vec{H}_{k}|\right)d\mathscr{H}^{1}+C|z|^{d_{k}}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}\left|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\right|d\mathscr{H}^{1}+C|z|^{d_{k}}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|d\mathscr{H}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq C+|z|^{d_{k}}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\widetilde{W}_{k}|d\mathscr{H}^{1}+C|z|^{d_{k}}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|d\mathscr{H}^{1}\,.

By the
\mathrm{L}^{2,1}/\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}
duality in dimension
1
and trace theory, since
\widetilde{W}_{k}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\widetilde{W}_{k}|d\mathscr{H}^{1}\leq\left\|1\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\left\|\widetilde{W}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\partial B(0,|z|))}\leq C|z|\left\|\widetilde{W}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}))}\leq C|z|\,.

Since
d_{k}>-1+\varepsilon
, we deduce by the
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
estimate for the means of
\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})
that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})(z)|\leq C+C|z|^{d_{k}}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|d\mathscr{H}^{1}\,.

Integrating this identity and using
d_{k}>-1+\varepsilon
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|d\mathscr{H}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi C|z|+2\pi C|z|^{1+d_{k}}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|d\mathscr{H}^{1}

\displaystyle\leq C|z|+C(\alpha_{0}R_{k})^{\varepsilon}\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|d\mathscr{H}^{1},

which shows that for
\alpha_{0}>0
small enough, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B(0,|z|)}|\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})|d\mathscr{H}^{1}\leq C|z|,\,.

We conclude that
\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
. A similar argument (easier since we have a
\partial_{z}
equation and not a
\nabla_{z}
one) finally yields that

\displaystyle\left\|S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/4))}+\left\|\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/4))}\leq C.

To complete the proof, recall from
[
38
]
that in
B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k})
it holds:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\left(\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\right)&=(-1)^{n}\star_{h}\left(\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\nabla^{\perp}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\right)-\star_{h}\left(\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\mathbin{\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt}\nabla^{\perp}(\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k}))\right)+\vec{G}_{1,k}\\
\Delta\left(\mathrm{Re}\,(S_{k})\right)&=\langle\nabla(\star_{h}\vec{n}_{k}),\nabla^{\perp}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\rangle+G_{2,k}\end{aligned}\right.

for some
\vec{G}_{1,k}
and
G_{2,k}
which are bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
, for all
1\leq p<2
.

Recall also the following slight variant (see
[
33
]
) from a Lemma of
[
25
]
.

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
R_{0}>0
be the constant of Lemma
3.18
.
Let
0<16r<R<R_{0}
,
\Omega=B(0,R)\setminus\overline{B}(0,r)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
a,b:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
\nabla a\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)
and
\nabla b\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, and
\varphi:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a solution of

\displaystyle\Delta\varphi=\nabla a\cdot\nabla^{\perp}b\quad\text{in}\;\ \Omega.

Assume that
\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)}<\infty
. Then there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that for all
\left(\dfrac{r}{R}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\alpha<\dfrac{1}{4}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B_{\alpha R}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}r}(0))}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(B_{\alpha R}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}r}(0))}\leq C_{1}\left(\left\|\nabla a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla b\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)}\right).

From Lemma
3.19
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}+\left\|\nabla^{2}S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/2))}\leq C.

Since
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L}_{k})\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
we deduce that
e^{\lambda_{k}}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L}_{k})\in\mathrm{L}^{2+\varepsilon}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))
. Using the identity

\displaystyle e^{\lambda_{k}}\vec{H}_{k}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\nabla_{z}\vec{R}_{k}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}e^{-\lambda_{k}}\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}_{k}\right)-\frac{1}{2}e^{\lambda_{k}}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L}_{k})

\displaystyle\quad-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(ie^{-\lambda_{k}}\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}_{k}\,\partial_{z}S_{k}\right)+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}_{k},\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L}_{k})\rangle e^{-\lambda}\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}_{k}\right)\,,

we finally get

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda_{k}}\vec{H}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}/4))}\leq C

which concludes the proof of Theorem
3.2
.

\Box

4
Weak
\varepsilon
-regularity for Willmore immersions with values into manifolds

By
[
38
, Lemma
3.2
and Theorem
3.1
]
, the following identities are satisfied for any smooth immersion
\vec{\Phi}:\Sigma\rightarrow(M^{m},h)

\displaystyle\vec{Y}=i\left(\nabla_{z}\vec{H}-3\,\nabla_{z}^{\perp}\vec{H}-i\star_{h}(\nabla_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H})\right)

\displaystyle=-2i\left(\nabla_{z}^{\perp}\vec{H}+\langle\vec{H},\vec{H}_{0}\rangle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle 4e^{-2\lambda}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\nabla_{\overline{z}}\left(\nabla_{z}^{\perp}\vec{H}+\langle\vec{H},\vec{H}_{0}\rangle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{g}^{\perp}\vec{H}-2|\vec{H}|^{2}\vec{H}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{H})+8\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle R(\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},\vec{e}_{z})\vec{e}_{z},\vec{H}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\right).

We deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,(\nabla_{\overline{z}}\vec{Y})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Im}\,\left(-2i\nabla_{\overline{z}}\left(\nabla_{z}^{\perp}\vec{H}+\langle\vec{H},\vec{H}_{0}\rangle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}\right)\right)=-2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\nabla_{\overline{z}}\left(\nabla_{z}^{\perp}\vec{H}+\langle\vec{H},\vec{H}_{0}\rangle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}\left(\Delta_{g}^{\perp}\vec{H}-2|\vec{H}|^{2}\vec{H}+\mathscr{A}(\vec{H})\right)-4e^{2\lambda}\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle R(\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},\vec{e}_{z})\vec{e}_{z},\vec{H}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\right).

Assuming that
\vec{\Phi}
is a Willmore immersion, from (
1.1
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,(\nabla_{\overline{z}}\vec{Y})=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}\left(\mathscr{R}_{1}^{\perp}(\vec{H})-2\,\widetilde{K}_{h}\,\vec{H}+2\,\mathscr{R}_{2}(d\vec{\Phi})+(DR)(d\vec{\Phi})-8\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle R(\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},\vec{e}_{z})\vec{e}_{z},\vec{H}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\right)\right).

(4.1)

As before, let
f:\mathbb{C}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{m}
be the linear map such that for all
X\in C^{\infty}(B(0,1),\mathbb{C}^{m})
, it holds

\displaystyle\nabla_{z}\vec{X}=\partial_{z}\vec{X}+f(\vec{X})=\partial_{z}\vec{X}+\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\gamma^{j}_{l}\vec{X}^{l}\right)_{1\leq j\leq m}\,,

where

\gamma^{j}_{l}=\sum_{q=1}^{m}\Gamma_{l,q}^{j}\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}^{q}

and
\Gamma_{l,q}^{j}
are the Christoffel symbols of the ambient space
(M^{m},h)
.

Likewise, there exists linear maps
F:\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{C}^{m}\rightarrow\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{C}^{m}
and
G:\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{C}^{m}\rightarrow\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{C}^{m}
(notice that
G=f
is
m=3
) such that for all
\vec{Y}\in C^{\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\vec{Z}\in C^{\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{C}^{m})
it holds:

\displaystyle\nabla_{z}\vec{Y}=\partial_{z}\vec{Y}+F(\vec{Y})\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla_{z}\vec{Z}=\partial_{z}\vec{Z}+G(\vec{Z})\,.

Notice that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,(\nabla_{\overline{z}}\vec{Y})=\mathrm{Im}\,(\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{Y}+\overline{f_{k}}(\vec{Y})),

that

\displaystyle\vec{Y}=i\left(\partial_{z}\vec{H}-3\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(\partial_{z}\vec{H}\right)-i\star_{h}\left(\partial_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-2f_{k}(\vec{H})-i\star_{h}\left(G(\vec{n})\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right).

and that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\left(\vec{Y}\right)\right)=-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\left(\partial_{z}\vec{H}-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\partial_{z}\vec{H})-i\,\star_{h}(\partial_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H})\right)\right)+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\left(f(\vec{H})-i\star_{h}\left(G(\vec{n})\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)\right).

We immediately get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\left(\partial_{z}\vec{H}-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(\partial_{z}\vec{H}\right)\right)\right)=\frac{1}{4}\dive\left(\nabla\vec{H}-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\left(-i\star_{h}\left(\partial_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(-\frac{i}{4}\star_{h}\left(\Delta\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(-i\,\star_{h}\left(\partial_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{H}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\nabla\vec{H}\right)=\frac{1}{4}\dive\left(\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right).

Finally, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\left(\vec{Y}\right)\right)=-\frac{1}{4}\dive\left(\nabla\vec{H}-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})+\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\left(f(\vec{H})-i\ast_{h}\left(G(\vec{n})\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)\right).

Now, we have

\displaystyle\vec{Y}

\displaystyle=i\left(\partial_{z}\vec{H}-3\pi_{\vec{n}}(\partial_{z}\vec{H})-2f_{k}(\vec{H})-i\star(\partial_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H})-i\star_{h}\left(G(\vec{n})\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=i\left(-2\,\partial_{z}\vec{H}+3\left(\partial_{z}\pi_{\vec{n}}\right)\vec{H}-2f(\vec{H})-i\star(\partial_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H})-i\star_{h}\left(G(\vec{n})\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right).

We compute

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\overline{f}\left((\partial_{z}\pi_{\vec{n}})\vec{H}\right)\right)_{j}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\langle\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q},\nabla\pi_{\vec{n}}\rangle\vec{H}_{j}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\vec{A}_{2}(\vec{n})\cdot\vec{H}\right)_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,\left(-i\,\overline{f}\left(f(\vec{H})\right)\right)_{j}=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}_{q}f(\vec{H})_{l}\right)=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\Gamma_{l,q^{\prime}}^{l^{\prime}}\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}_{q}\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}_{q^{\prime}}\vec{H}_{l^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\Gamma_{l,q^{\prime}}^{l^{\prime}}\langle\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q},\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q^{\prime}}\rangle\vec{H}_{l^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\vec{A}_{3}\cdot\vec{H}\right)_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\overline{f}\left(\star_{h}\left(\partial_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)\right)_{j}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\left\langle\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q},\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)_{l}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\vec{A}_{4}\cdot\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\overline{f}\left(\star_{h}\left(G(\vec{n})\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)\right)=\frac{1}{4}\vec{A}_{4}\cdot(\vec{W}\wedge\vec{H}).

Likewise, we have for all
1\leq j\leq n

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\overline{f}(-2i\,\partial_{z}\vec{H})\right)_{j}=\mathrm{Im}\,\left(-2i\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}_{q}\partial_{z}\vec{H}_{l}\right)=-2\,\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}_{q}\partial_{z}\vec{H}_{l}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{l=1}^{n}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\langle\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q},\nabla\vec{H}_{l}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\dive\left(\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q}\,\vec{H}_{l}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\langle\nabla\Gamma_{j,q}^{l},\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q}\rangle\vec{H}_{l}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\Delta\vec{\Phi}_{q}\,\vec{H}_{l}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\dive\left(\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q}\,\vec{H}_{l}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\langle\nabla\Gamma_{j,q}^{l},\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q}\rangle\vec{H}_{l}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}e^{2\lambda}\vec{H}_{q}\vec{H}_{l}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\dive\left(\vec{A}_{0}\cdot\vec{H}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}+\frac{1}{4}B(\vec{H},\vec{H}),

where
\vec{A}_{0},\vec{A}_{1}\in M_{n}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
B
is a bilinear map, such that for some universal constant
C_{2}=C_{2}(n,h)
, have

\displaystyle|\vec{A}_{0}|+|\vec{A}_{1}|+\sqrt{\|B\|}\leq C_{2}e^{\lambda}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}f(\vec{H})\right)=\frac{1}{4}\dive\left(\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}^{l}\nabla\vec{\Phi}_{q}\vec{H}_{l}\right)_{1\leq j\leq n}=\frac{1}{4}\dive\left(\vec{A}_{0}\cdot\vec{H}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\partial_{\overline{z}}\left(-i\star_{h}\left(G(\vec{n})\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\dive\left(\star_{h}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n-2}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j,q}\left(\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\vec{n}_{k-1}\wedge\nabla^{\perp}\vec{\Phi}_{q}\vec{n}_{k,l}\right)\wedge\vec{n}_{k+1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\vec{n}_{n-2}\right)\wedge\vec{H}\right)_{1\leq j\leq n}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\dive\left(\star_{h}\left(\vec{V}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)

where
\vec{V}\in\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Finally, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &|\vec{A}_{0}|+|\vec{A}_{1}|+|\vec{A}_{4}|+\sqrt{\|B\|}+|\vec{V}|+|\vec{W}|\leq Ce^{\lambda}\\
&|\vec{A}_{2}(\vec{n})|\leq Ce^{\lambda}|\nabla\vec{n}|\\
&|\vec{A}_{3}|\leq Ce^{2\lambda}\,,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.2)

for some
C=C(m,h)>0
.
We deduce that

\displaystyle\dive\left(\nabla\vec{H}-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})+\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)+\vec{A}_{0}\cdot\vec{H}+\star_{h}(\vec{V}\wedge\vec{H})\right)

\displaystyle=-4\mathrm{Im}\,(\nabla_{\overline{z}}\vec{Y})

\displaystyle=-2e^{2\lambda}\left(\mathscr{R}_{1}^{\perp}(\vec{H})-2\,\widetilde{K}_{h}\,\vec{H}+2\,\mathscr{R}_{2}(d\vec{\Phi})+(DR)(d\vec{\Phi})-8\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle R(\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},\vec{e}_{z})\vec{e}_{z},\vec{H}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}-{3}\,\vec{A}_{2}(\vec{n})\cdot\vec{H}-2\,\vec{A}_{3}\cdot\vec{H}-\vec{A}_{4}\cdot\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-\vec{A}_{5}\cdot\star_{h}\left(\vec{W}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-B(\vec{H},\vec{H}).

(4.3)

The bounds (
4.2
) give

\displaystyle\bigg|-2e^{2\lambda}\left(\mathscr{R}_{1}^{\perp}(\vec{H})-2\,\widetilde{K}_{h}\,\vec{H}+2\,\mathscr{R}_{2}(d\vec{\Phi})+(DR)(d\vec{\Phi})-8\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle R(\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},\vec{e}_{z})\vec{e}_{z},\vec{H}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}-{3}\,\vec{A}_{2}(\vec{n})\cdot\vec{H}-2\,\vec{A}_{3}\cdot\vec{H}-\vec{A}_{4}\cdot\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-\vec{A}_{5}\cdot\star_{h}\left(\vec{W}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-B(\vec{H},\vec{H})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\left(e^{\lambda}+e^{2\lambda}+e^{\lambda}|\vec{H}|+e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|+|\nabla\vec{n}|e^{\lambda}|\vec{H}|+e^{2\lambda}|\vec{H}|^{2}\right)\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1)).

Following the strategy of
[
48
]
, we next study the linear operator
\mathscr{L}_{\vec{n}}
defined for all vector-fields
\vec{u}:B(0,1)\rightarrow TM^{m}
by

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\vec{n}}\vec{u}=\Delta\vec{u}+\dive\left(-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})+\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{u}\right)+\vec{A}_{0}\cdot\vec{u}+\star_{h}(\vec{V}\wedge\vec{u})\right).

(4.4)

Compared to
[
48
]
,
\mathscr{L}_{\vec{n}}
only differs by lower-order terms that are in
\mathrm{L}^{2}
(if
\lambda\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))
and
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))
), and one checks that it is an elliptic operator for vector-fields with values into
M^{m}
.
It will be convenient to use Nash embedding to isometrically embed all the objects in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. By (
2.1
), calling
\widetilde{\vec{n}}:\Sigma\rightarrow\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}
the induced unit normal of the immersion
\iota\circ\vec{\Phi}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{\widetilde{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})=\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H}).

Furthermore, using the function
\vec{V}_{0}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n}
defined by

\displaystyle\vec{V}_{0}=\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\vec{e}_{1}\wedge\vec{e}_{2}\wedge\vec{n}\right)=\vec{n}_{\iota}\circ\vec{\Phi},

where
\vec{n}_{\iota}:M^{m}\rightarrow\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the unit normal of the inclusion
\iota:M^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
we deduce that there exist
\vec{A}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1),M_{n}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\vec{V}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that the operator
\mathscr{L}
defined for all
\vec{u}\in C^{\infty}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
by

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{1}\vec{u}=\Delta\vec{u}+\dive\left(-3\,\pi_{\widetilde{\vec{n}}}(\nabla\vec{u})+\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{0}\wedge\vec{u}\right)+\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{u}+\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)\,,

satisfies for all
\vec{u}\in\Gamma(TM^{m})
the identity

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{1}(\vec{u})=\mathscr{L}_{\vec{n}}(\vec{u}).

However, we need to have the critical part of
\mathscr{L}_{1}
self-adjoint, so we replace it by

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}\vec{u}=\Delta\vec{u}+\dive\left(-3\,\pi_{\widetilde{\vec{n}}}(\nabla\vec{u})+\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{0}\wedge\vec{u}\right)+\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{u}+\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)+\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)

for some
\vec{V}_{2}
to be determined later. The
critical part
of
\mathscr{L}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{u}=\Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\widetilde{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{0}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)+\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right).

Making the same computation as in the proof of of
[
48
, Lemma A.
1
]
(see (A.
28
)), we deduce that for all
\vec{v}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
, it holds

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}\langle\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{u},\vec{v}\rangle dx=\int_{B(0,1)}\bigg(\left\langle\vec{u},\Delta\vec{v}\right\rangle-3\,\left\langle\vec{u},\dive\left(\pi_{\widetilde{\vec{n}}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)\right\rangle-\left\langle\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{0}\wedge\vec{u}\right),\nabla\vec{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right),\vec{v}\right\rangle\bigg)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,1)}\bigg(\left\langle\vec{u},\Delta\vec{v}\right\rangle-3\,\left\langle\vec{u},\dive\left(\pi_{\widetilde{\vec{n}}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)\right\rangle+\left\langle\vec{u},\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{0}\wedge\vec{v}\right)\right\rangle-\left\langle\vec{u},\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{2}\wedge\vec{v}\right)\right\rangle\bigg)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,1)}\left(\left\langle\vec{u},\Delta\vec{v}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\widetilde{\vec{n}}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{V}_{0}\wedge\nabla\vec{v}\right)-\star_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge(\nabla\vec{V}_{0}+\vec{V}_{2})\wedge\vec{v}\right)\right)\right\rangle\right)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,1)}\langle\vec{u},\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{v}\rangle dx

(4.5)

if and only if

\displaystyle\vec{V}_{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\nabla\vec{V}_{0}\,.

Notice that
\vec{n}_{\iota}\in C^{\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, since
(M^{m},h)
is smooth. Assuming without loss of generality that
\vec{\Phi}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),M^{m})
, this implies that
\nabla\vec{V}_{0}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))
and that
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathscr{L}_{0}
are well-defined in the distributional sense for all
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
since

\displaystyle\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\nabla\vec{V}_{0}\wedge\vec{u}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1)).

Next, we will study the properties of the operator
\mathscr{L}
.
Applying
[
48
, Lemma A.
2
and Lemma A.4]
directly to the self-adjoint operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}
, one easily checks that the arguments
[
48
, Lemma A.
1
and Lemma A.3]
can be adapted to
\mathscr{L}
. The point is that
\mathscr{L}
is
not
self-adjoint, however
\mathscr{L}^{-1}:\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})\rightarrow\mathrm{W}^{1,2}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
is still a compact operator on
\mathrm{L}^{2}
and this is the key to make the strategy work. Moreover, using that
\mathscr{L}_{0}
is indeed self-adjoint, one can adapt
[
48
, Lemma A.
8
]
.

Lemma 4.1
.

For all
0<C_{1}<\infty
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(n)>0
and
C_{0}=C_{0}(n)
with the following property. Let
2\leq m\leq n
. For all
\vec{n}_{1}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
letting
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, assume that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{4}dx\leq C_{1}\,.

(4.6)

Then, for all
\vec{f}\in\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
, there exists a unique map
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u})\right)-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)&=\vec{f}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{u}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\end{aligned}\right.

(4.7)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{0}\left\|\vec{f}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1))}.

(4.8)

Furthermore, the operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}^{-1}:\vec{f}\rightarrow\vec{u}
is a self-adjoint and compact operator from
\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
into itself.

Proof.

First define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{0}\vec{u}=\Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u})\right).

Under the assumption (
4.1
), by the proofs of
[
48
, Lemma A.
1
and Lemma A.
2
]
, we deduce that there exists a unique
\vec{u}_{0}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{0}\vec{u}_{0}=\vec{f}
and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{u}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{0}\left\|\vec{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1))}.

Furthermore, by the proofs of
[
48
, Lemma A.
3
and Lemma A.
4
]
, there exists a unique
\vec{v}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{0}\vec{v}=\vec{g}
and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{v}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{0}\left\|\vec{g}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1))}.

Now, we will use a fixed point argument to conclude. We want to construct a function
\vec{u}
such that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{u}=\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{0}\vec{u}_{0}

or, equivalently, such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{0}(\vec{u}-\vec{u}_{0})=\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right).

By induction on
k\geq 1
, we define
\vec{u}_{k}
to be the unique
\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
function such that

\displaystyle\mathscr{\mathscr{L}}_{0}\vec{u}_{k}=\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}_{k-1}\right).

Using that
\displaystyle\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,1))\hookrightarrow\bigcap_{p<\infty}\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,1))
, by the Sobolev inequality and interpolation, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{u}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{0}\left\|\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}_{k-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B(0,1))}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\left\|\vec{u}_{k-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{0}C_{1}C_{S}\varepsilon_{0}\left\|\nabla\vec{u}_{k-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))},

which implies that choosing
\varepsilon_{0}=\min\left\{\varepsilon_{1},\frac{1}{2C_{0}C_{1}C_{S}}\right\}
, the series
\sum_{k=0}^{n}\vec{u}_{k}
converges to some limit

\displaystyle\vec{u}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\vec{u}_{k}

which satisfies

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{0}\vec{u}=\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{0}\vec{u}_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}_{k-1}\right)=\vec{f}+\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right),

so that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{u}=\vec{f}.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq 2\left\|\nabla\vec{u}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq 2C_{0}\left\|\vec{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1))}

which implies the needed estimate up to replacing
C_{0}
by
2C_{0}
in the statement of the theorem. Finally, the self-adjointness follows from the above computations in (
4
).
∎

Remark 4.2
.

Notice that in fact

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\in\bigcap_{p<2}\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,1))

so we could also do the argument using Calderón-Zgymund estimates and get a
\mathrm{W}^{1,2}_{0}
solution to the original equation, but a
\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}
regularity suffices to our purposes.

Now, we easily obtain the following extension of Lemma
4.1
.

Lemma 4.3
.

For all
0<C_{1}<\infty
and
2<p<\infty
, there exists
\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon_{1}(p,n)>0
and
C_{1}=C_{1}(n)
with the following property. Let
2\leq m\leq n
. For all
\vec{n}_{1}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})

\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
\vec{A}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1),M_{n}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\vec{V}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{2})
letting
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, assume that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}\left(|\vec{A}_{1}|^{p}+|\vec{V}_{1}|^{p}\right)dx\leq\varepsilon_{1},\\
&\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{4}dx\leq C_{1}\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.9)

Then, for all
\vec{f}\in\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
, there exists a unique map
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u})\right)\qquad\qquad&\\
-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)+\dive\left(\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{u}+\star\left(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)&=\vec{f}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{u}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\end{aligned}\right.

(4.10)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}\left\|\vec{f}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1))}.

(4.11)

Proof.

By Lemma
4.1
, we deduce that there exists
\vec{u}_{0}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1))
such that
\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{u}_{0}=\vec{f}
. Then notice that by Hölder’s inequality and the Sobolev inequality, we have for all
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})

\displaystyle\left\|\dive\left(\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{u}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}\left\|\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}\left\|\vec{A}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,1))}\left\|\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}(B(0,1))}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{p}\left\|\vec{A}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,1))}\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}C_{p}\varepsilon_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}}\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}.

Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\dive\left(\star\left(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}C_{p}\varepsilon_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}}\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}.

Therefore, we deduce that for
\varepsilon_{1}=\min\left\{\varepsilon_{0},\frac{1}{(4C_{1}C_{p})^{p}}\right\}
, we can use the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
to deduce that there exists
\vec{u}
such that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}\vec{u}=\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{u}_{0}=\vec{f}

and the estimate follows as above.
∎

Adapting
mutadis mutandis
the above results and the proof of
[
48
, Lemma A.
3
]
, we deduce the following two lemmas.

Lemma 4.4
.

For all
0<C_{1}<\infty
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(n)>0
and
C_{0}=C_{0}(n)
with the following property. Let
2\leq m\leq n
. For all
\vec{n}_{1}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
letting
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, assume that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{4}dx\leq C_{1}\,.

(4.12)

Then for all
\vec{f}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
, there exists a unique map
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u})\right)-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)&=\vec{f}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{u}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\end{aligned}\right.

(4.13)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{0}\left\|\vec{f}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1))}.

(4.14)

Lemma 4.5
.

For all
0<C_{1}<\infty
and
2<p<\infty
, there exists
\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon_{1}(p,n)>0
and
C_{1}=C_{1}(n)
with the following property. Let
2\leq m\leq n
. For all
\vec{n}_{1}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})

\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
\vec{A}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1),M_{n}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\vec{V}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{2})
letting
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, assume that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}\left(|\vec{A}_{1}|^{p}+|\vec{V}_{1}|^{p}\right)dx\leq\varepsilon_{1},\\
&\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{4}dx\leq C_{1}\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.15)

Then, for all
\vec{f}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
, there exists a unique map
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u})\right)\qquad\qquad&\\
-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)+\dive\left(\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{u}+\star\left(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)&=\vec{f}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{u}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\end{aligned}\right.

(4.16)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}\left\|\vec{f}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1))}.

(4.17)

Now, we can show in a similar way the improved regularity for eigenvectors of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

For all
0<C_{1}<\infty
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(n)>0
and
C_{0}=C_{0}(n)
with the following property. Let
2\leq m\leq n
. For all
\vec{n}_{1}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,1)}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
letting
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, assume that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}.

(4.18)

Assume that
\vec{\varphi}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
is an eigen-vector of
\mathscr{L}_{0}

i.e.
that there exists
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{\varphi}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{\varphi})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{\varphi})\right)-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{\varphi}\right)&=\lambda\,\vec{\varphi}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{\varphi}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.19)

Then

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\vec{\varphi}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{0}\left\|\nabla\vec{\varphi}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}.

(4.20)

Proof.

The first part of the proof goes as in
[
48
]
and we deduce by the Sobolev embedding

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}^{1,2}_{0}(B(0,1))\hookrightarrow\bigcap_{p<\infty}\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,1))

that

\displaystyle\vec{\varphi}\in\bigcap_{p<\infty}\mathrm{W}^{1,p}_{0}(B(0,1)).

Now, as in
[
48
]
, let
x_{0}\in B(0,1)
. Since
\nabla\vec{n}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))
and
\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{L}^{4}(B(0,1))
, for all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\rho>0
, there exists
\rho>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(x_{0},\rho))}+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(x_{0},\rho))}\leq\varepsilon.

We now define
\vec{u}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
by

\displaystyle\vec{u}(x)=\vec{\varphi}(x_{0}+\rho\,x)\eta(x)=\vec{\psi}(x)\,\eta(x),

where
\eta
is a smooth cut-off function such that
\eta=1
on
B(0,\frac{1}{2})
. Then we compute (without changing notations for the rescaled versions of
\vec{n}
)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{u}

\displaystyle=\lambda\vec{u}+2\nabla\vec{\psi}\cdot\nabla\eta+\vec{\psi}\Delta\eta-3\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\vec{\psi})\nabla\eta+\eta\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{\psi})\right)+3\,\eta\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{\psi})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{\psi}\right)\cdot\nabla\eta+\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{\psi}\right)\Delta\eta

\displaystyle=\lambda\,\vec{u}+2\nabla\vec{\psi}\cdot\nabla\eta+\vec{\psi}\Delta\eta-3\,\nabla\pi_{\vec{n}}(\vec{\psi})\cdot\nabla\eta-6\,\dive(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{\psi}))\cdot\nabla\eta-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\vec{\psi})\Delta\eta

\displaystyle\qquad+\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{\psi}\right)\cdot\nabla\eta+\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{\psi}\right)\Delta\eta

\displaystyle=\vec{f}.

Since
\nabla\vec{n}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))
,
\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))
while
\vec{\psi}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,p}_{0}(B(0,1))
for all
p<\infty
(which implies in particular that
\vec{\psi}\in C^{0}(B(0,1))
), we deduce that
\vec{f}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))
. Therefore,
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,p}_{0}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
(for all
p<\infty
) solves the system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{u}&=\vec{f}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{u}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

(4.21)

Following
[
48
]
, introduce the Hodge decomposition

\displaystyle\nabla\vec{u}-3\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})=\nabla\vec{A}+\nabla^{\perp}\vec{B},

where
\vec{A}=0
and
\partial_{\nu}\vec{B}=0
on
\partial B(0,1)
. Then we directly have

\displaystyle\Delta\vec{A}=\Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)=-\dive\left(\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)+\vec{f}

so that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{A}&=-\dive\left(\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)+\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)+\vec{f}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{A}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

Likewise, we directly obtain

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{B}&=3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{u})\right)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{B}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

By Calderón-Zygmund estimates, interpolation, and the Sobolev embedding
\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,1)}_{0}(B(0,1))\hookrightarrow C^{0}(B(0,1))
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{A}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\vec{A}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\leq C\left\|\Delta\vec{A}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\left(\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}\left\|\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}+\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}\right)+\left\|\vec{f}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{B}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\vec{B}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\leq C\left\|\Delta\vec{B}\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))}\left\|\nabla\vec{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}

From this point we can follow verbatim the proof of
[
48
, Lemma A.
7
]
to conclude.
∎

Finally, we deduce the counterpart of
[
48
, Lemma A.
8
]
for the operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

For all
0<C_{1}<\infty
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(n)>0
and
C_{0}=C_{0}(n)
with the following property. Let
2\leq m\leq n
. For all
\vec{n}_{1}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,1)}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
letting
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, assume that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}.

(4.22)

Assume that
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
and that
\nabla\vec{u}
is the sum of a
compactly supported distribution in
B(0,1)
and of a function in
\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))
. Suppose that
\vec{u}
satisfies the following equation in the distributional sense

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u})\right)-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{u}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

(4.23)

Then
\vec{u}=0
on
B(0,1)
.

Proof.

We can follow verbatim the proof of
[
48
, Lemma A.
8
]
since only the self-adjointness of the operator and the regularity result of Lemma
4.6
are used.
∎

Finally, we deduce that Lemma
4.7
holds with
\mathscr{L}_{0}
replaced by
\mathscr{L}
.

Lemma 4.8
.

For all
0<C_{1}<\infty
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(n)>0
and
C_{0}=C_{0}(n)
with the following property. Let
2\leq m\leq n
. For all
\vec{n}_{1}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,1)}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{m-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
letting
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, assume that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{1}|^{2}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}.

(4.24)

Assume that
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R}^{n})
and that
\nabla\vec{u}
is the sum of a
compactly supported distribution in
B(0,1)
and of a function in
\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))
. Suppose that
\vec{u}
satisfies the following equation in the distributional sense

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{u}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{u})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u})\right)\qquad\qquad&\\
-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{u}\right)+\dive\left(\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{u}+\star\left(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{u}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\end{aligned}\right.

(4.25)

Then
\vec{u}=0
on
B(0,1)
.

Proof.

Since
\vec{u}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))
and
\vec{A}_{1},\vec{V}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\vec{f}=\dive\left(\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{u}+\star\left(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{u}\right)\right)\in\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1)).

Using Lemma
4.1
, we deduce that there exists a unique
\vec{v}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}_{0}(B(0,1))
such that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}\vec{v}=\vec{f}.

Therefore, we get that
\vec{w}=\vec{u}-\vec{v}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\vec{w}-3\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{w})\right)+\dive\left(\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{w})\right)-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{w}\right)&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{w}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

Since
\vec{v}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))
, we deduce that
\nabla\vec{w}
is also the sum of a compactly supported distribution and of a
\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))
function. Therefore, by Lemma
4.7
, we obtain that
\vec{w}=0
,
i.e.

\vec{u}=\vec{v}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1))
.
Using the uniqueness of the weak solution in
\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1))
of the Dirichlet problem associated to
\mathscr{L}
(see Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.5
), we conclude that
\vec{u}=0
.
∎

Finally, we can prove the
\varepsilon
-regularity result (see
[
48
, Theorem I.5]
for Willmore immersions in the Euclidean space).

Theorem 4.9
.

Let
(M^{m},h)
be a smooth closed Riemannian manifold that we assume isometrically embedded in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.. There exists constants
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
\left\{C_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\infty)
with the following property. Let
\vec{\Phi}:B(0,1)\rightarrow(M^{m},h)
be a conformal, Lipschitz ; denote with
\vec{n}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\Lambda^{m-2}TM^{m}
the Gauss map associated to
\vec{\Phi}
and assume that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}|^{2}dx+\mathrm{Area}(\vec{\Phi}(B(0,1)))\leq\varepsilon_{0}\,.

(4.26)

Then, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{k}\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))}^{2}\leq C_{k}\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}|^{2}dx.

Proof.

Step 1
. Proof that
\vec{\Phi}\in\mathrm{W}^{2,(2,1)}_{\mathrm{loc}}(B(0,1))
.

Using
[
38
, Lemma
6.1
and
6.2
]
, we deduce that there exists
\vec{L}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1),\mathbb{C}^{n})
such that
\nabla\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L})\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,1))
and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla_{z}\vec{L}&=\vec{Y}=i\left(\nabla_{z}\vec{H}-3\,\nabla_{z}^{\perp}\vec{H}-i\star_{h}(\nabla_{z}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H})\right)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L})&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

Likewise, there exists
S\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,1),\mathbb{C})
such that
\mathrm{Im}\,(S)\in\mathrm{W}^{2,q}(B(0,1),\mathbb{R})
for all
q<2
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{z}S&=\langle\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi},\overline{\vec{L}}\rangle\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\mathrm{Im}\,(S)&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\end{aligned}\right.

and
\vec{R}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{C}^{n})
such that
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R})\in\mathrm{W}^{2,q}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
q<2
and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla_{z}\vec{R}&=\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}\wedge\overline{\vec{L}}-2i\,\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}\wedge\vec{H}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\vec{R}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

Furthermore, using
[
38
, Proposition
6.1
]
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta\mathrm{Re}\,(S)&=\langle\nabla(\star_{h}\vec{n}),\nabla^{\perp}\vec{R}\rangle+F\qquad\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
\Delta\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R})&=(-1)^{m}\star_{h}\left(\nabla\vec{n}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\nabla^{\perp}\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R})\right)-\star_{h}\langle\nabla\vec{n},\nabla^{\perp}\mathrm{Re}\,(S)\rangle+\vec{G}\qquad\text{in}\;\,B(0,1),\end{aligned}\right.

where
F\in\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,1))
for all
p<2
and
\vec{G}\in\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
p<2
.
Therefore, we can perform a decomposition
\mathrm{Re}\,(S)=u+v+w
, where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta u&=\langle\nabla\left(\star_{h}\vec{n}\right),\nabla^{\perp}\vec{R}\rangle\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1)\,\end{aligned}\right.\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta v&=F\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,B(0,1)\\
v&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial B(0,1).\end{aligned}\right.

Since
\vec{S},\vec{R}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,1))
and
\vec{n}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,1))
, by the
\mathrm{L}^{2}
-
\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}
Wente estimate (
[
14
, Théorème
3.4.5
]
), we deduce that
\nabla u\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))
and that there exists a universal constant
C_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{1}\left\|\nabla\vec{R}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}.

By Calderón-Zygmund estimates, we get that
v\in\mathrm{W}^{2,q}(B(0,1))
for all
q<2
. Also, since
w
is harmonic, we obtain (see
[
4
]
and
[
26
]
, or
[
33
, Lemma
2.2
and
2.3
]
) that for all
r<1
there exists
C_{r}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla w\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,r))}+\left\|\nabla^{2}w\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,r))}\leq C_{r}\left\|\nabla w\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}.

We deduce that
\mathrm{Re}\,(S)\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,r))
for all
r<1
, and a similar decomposition also yields that
\mathrm{Re}\,(\vec{R})\in\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(B(0,r))
for all
r<1
. Using the Coifman-Lions-Meyer-Semmes compensation compactness result
[
8
]
, we deduce that
\vec{R},\vec{S}\in\mathrm{W}^{2,1}(B(0,r))
for all
r<1
which implies in particular that
\vec{R},\vec{S}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,1)}\cap C^{0}(B(0,r))
for all
r<1
.

By
[
38
, Identity (6.52)]
, we have

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda}\vec{H}=-\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\nabla_{z}\vec{R}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}e^{2\lambda}\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L})-\mathrm{Re}\,\left(i\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}\,\partial_{z}S\right)+\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi},\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L})\rangle\partial_{\overline{z}}\vec{\Phi}\right)\,.

(4.27)

Using that
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L})\in\mathrm{L}^{p}(B(0,1))
for all
p<\infty
, we deduce that

\displaystyle e^{\lambda}\vec{H}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,r))\qquad\text{for all}\;\,r<1.

Recalling that
\Delta\vec{\Phi}=e^{2\lambda}\vec{H}
and using that
\lambda\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))
, by Calderón-Zygmund estimates we deduce that
\vec{\Phi}\in\mathrm{W}^{2,(2,1)}(B(0,r))
for all
r<1
, which implies in particular that
\nabla\vec{n}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(B(0,1))
.

Step 2
. Proof that
\vec{H}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))
.

To simplify notations, write
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}
and
\vec{n}=\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}
, where
\vec{n}_{2}=\vec{n}_{\iota}\circ\vec{\Phi}
. Since
M^{m}
is a smooth compact submanifold of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have that
\vec{n}_{\iota}\in C^{\infty}(M^{m},T\mathbb{R}^{n})
which implies that
\vec{n}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(B(0,1),\Lambda^{n-m}\mathbb{R}^{n})
, since
\vec{\Phi}
is Lipschitz.
Next we will apply the lemmas above, exploiting the assumption (
4.26
).

By (
4.2
), we easily check that the assumptions on
\vec{A}_{1}
and
\vec{V}_{1}
are satisfided in Lemmas
4.3
,
4.5
and
4.8
for some
p>2
small enough. Indeed, we have
|\vec{A}_{1}|+|\vec{V}_{1}|\leq C_{2}e^{\lambda}
, which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}\left(|\vec{A}_{1}|^{p}+|\vec{V}_{1}|^{p}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}C_{2}^{p}e^{(p-2)\left\|\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}}\int_{B(0,1)}e^{2\lambda}dx=C_{2}^{p}e^{(p-2)\left\|\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}}\mathrm{Area}(\vec{\Phi}(B(0,1)))

\displaystyle\leq 4C_{2}^{2}(1+C_{2})\varepsilon_{0}

for
p>2
small enough. However, that would make

\varepsilon_{0}
and
C_{k}
depend also on
\left\|\lambda\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}
. To circumvent that, in the argument below we will instead use the Lemmas
4.1
,
4.4
and
4.7
.
By (
4
), we have

\displaystyle\dive\left(\nabla\vec{H}-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})+\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)+\vec{A}_{0}\cdot\vec{H}+\star_{h}(\vec{V}\wedge\vec{H})\right)

\displaystyle=-2e^{2\lambda}\left(\mathscr{R}_{1}^{\perp}(\vec{H})-2\,\widetilde{K}_{h}\,\vec{H}+2\,\mathscr{R}_{2}(d\vec{\Phi})+(DR)(d\vec{\Phi})-8\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle R(\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},\vec{e}_{z})\vec{e}_{z},\vec{H}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}-{3}\,\vec{A}_{2}(\vec{n})\cdot\vec{H}-2\,\vec{A}_{3}\cdot\vec{H}-\vec{A}_{4}\cdot\star_{h}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-\vec{A}_{5}\cdot\star_{h}\left(\vec{W}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-B(\vec{H},\vec{H}),

which can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(\vec{H})=\Delta\vec{H}+\dive\left(-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})+\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{H}\right)+\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}+\star\left(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{H}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{H}\right)

\displaystyle=-2e^{2\lambda}\left(\mathscr{R}_{1}^{\perp}(\vec{H})-2\,\widetilde{K}_{h}\,\vec{H}+2\,\mathscr{R}_{2}(d\vec{\Phi})+(DR)(d\vec{\Phi})-8\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\langle R(\vec{e}_{\overline{z}},\vec{e}_{z})\vec{e}_{z},\vec{H}\rangle\vec{e}_{\overline{z}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}-{3}\,\vec{A}_{2}(\vec{n}_{1})\cdot\vec{H}-2\,\vec{A}_{3}\cdot\vec{H}-\vec{A}_{4}\cdot\star\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-\vec{A}_{5}\cdot\star\left(\widetilde{\vec{W}}\wedge\vec{H}\right)-B(\vec{H},\vec{H})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\nabla\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{H}\right)

\displaystyle=\vec{f}.

Notice that by hypothesis, we have
\vec{f}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1))
.

Since
\mathscr{L}(\vec{H})=\vec{f}
, if
\eta
is a smooth cut-off function
supported on
B(0,1)
such that
\eta=1
on
B(0,\frac{1}{2})
, we deduce that the following identity holds in the distributional sense

\displaystyle\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}\left(\nabla\left(\eta\vec{H}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\dive\left(\eta\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})+\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right)=\eta\,\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})\right)+\nabla\eta\cdot\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})+\dive\left(\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right)

\displaystyle=\eta\dive\left(\pi_{\vec{n}}(\nabla\vec{H})\right)+2\,\dive\left(\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right)-\Delta\,\eta\vec{H}-\nabla\eta\cdot\nabla\pi_{\vec{n}}(\vec{H}),

thus

\displaystyle\Delta(\eta\vec{H})=\eta\Delta\vec{H}+2\langle\nabla\eta,\nabla\vec{H}\rangle+\Delta\eta\,\vec{H}=\eta\Delta\vec{H}+2\,\dive\left(\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right)-\Delta\eta\vec{H}\,.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}(\eta\vec{H})

\displaystyle=\eta\vec{f}_{1}+2\,\dive\left(\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right)-\Delta\eta\,\vec{H}-3\left(2\,\dive\left(\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right)-\Delta\eta\vec{H}-\nabla\eta\,\cdot\nabla\pi_{\vec{n}}(\vec{H})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\langle\nabla\eta,\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{H})\rangle+\langle\nabla\eta,\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}\rangle+\langle\nabla\eta,\star(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{H})\rangle

\displaystyle=\eta\vec{f}+2\,\Delta\eta\,\vec{H}+3\nabla\eta\cdot\nabla\pi_{\vec{n}}(\vec{H})+\langle\nabla\eta,\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{H})\rangle+\langle\nabla\eta,\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}\rangle+\langle\nabla\eta,\star(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{H})\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad-4\,\dive\left(\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right).

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{0}(\eta\vec{H})=\mathscr{L}(\vec{H})-\dive\left(\eta\left(\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}+\star(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{H})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\eta\vec{f}+2\,\Delta\eta\,\vec{H}+3\nabla\eta\cdot\nabla\pi_{\vec{n}}(\vec{H})+\langle\nabla\eta,\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{H})\rangle+\langle\nabla\eta,\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}\rangle+\langle\nabla\eta,\star(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{H})\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-4\,\dive\left(\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right)-\dive\left(\eta\left(\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}+\star(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{H})\right)\right)\,.

Note that

\displaystyle\vec{f}_{1}=\eta\vec{f}+2\,\Delta\eta\,\vec{H}+3\nabla\eta\cdot\nabla\pi_{\vec{n}}(\vec{H})+\langle\nabla\eta,\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{1}\wedge\vec{n}_{2}\wedge\vec{H})\rangle+\langle\nabla\eta,\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}\rangle+\langle\nabla\eta,\star(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{H})\rangle

satisfies
\vec{f}_{1}\in\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1))
with the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{f}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{3}^{2}\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}||\vec{H}|dx\,.

(4.28)

Analogously,

\displaystyle\vec{f}_{2}=-4\,\dive\left(\nabla\eta\vec{H}\right)-\dive\left(\eta\left(\vec{A}_{1}\cdot\vec{H}+\star(\vec{V}_{1}\wedge\vec{H})\right)\right)\in\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1))

can be estimated by

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{f}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-1}(B(0,1))}\leq C_{3}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\vec{H}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.29)

Applying Lemma
4.1
and
4.4
(respectively with
\vec{f}=\vec{f}_{1}
and
\vec{f}=\vec{f}_{2}
), we obtain that there exist
\vec{u}_{1},\vec{u}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1))
such that
\mathscr{L}\vec{u}_{1}=\vec{f}_{1}
and
\mathscr{L}\vec{u}_{2}=\vec{f}_{2}
on
B(0,1)
and satisfying the estimates (thanks to (
4.28
) and (
4.29
))

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{u}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}+\left\|\nabla\vec{u}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}C_{3}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\vec{n}||\vec{H}|dx+\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\vec{H}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}C_{3}(1+\varepsilon_{0})\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\vec{H}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Invoking the uniqueness result of Lemma
4.7
, we deduce that
\eta\vec{H}=\vec{u}_{1}+\vec{u}_{2}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}_{0}(B(0,1))

and that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(\eta\vec{H})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\vec{H}|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We conclude that
\vec{H}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))
. Now, one can complete the proof by following verbatim the arguments in the Euclidean
\varepsilon
-regularity theorem
[
48
]
.
∎

5
\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}
quantization of energy

The goal of this section is to prove the following result, establishing the
\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}
quantization of energy.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
(M^{m},h)
be a closed
m
-dimensional Riemannian manifold.
There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
with the following property. Let
\left\{r_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}},\left\{R_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\infty)
be such that
\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}R_{k}\in(0,\infty)
,
r_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
and set
\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha R_{k}}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}r_{k}}(0)
for all
0<\alpha\leq 1
. Let
\left\{\vec{\Phi}_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}:B(0,R_{k})\rightarrow(M^{m},h)
be a sequence of Willmore immersions such that

\displaystyle\Lambda=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\left\|\nabla\lambda_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(B(0,R_{k}))}+\int_{B(0,R_{k})}|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}|^{2}dx+\mathrm{Area}(\vec{\Phi}_{k}(B(0,R_{k})))\right)<\infty\,,

and

\displaystyle\sup_{r_{k}<s<\frac{R_{k}}{2}}\int_{B_{2s}\setminus\overline{B}_{s}(0)}|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}|^{2}dx\leq\varepsilon_{0}.

Then

\displaystyle\limsup_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\||x|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}=0.

In particular, it holds

\displaystyle\limsup_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}=0.

Proof.

By the
\varepsilon
-regularity Theorem
4.9
, we deduce that

\displaystyle|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}(z)|^{2}\leq\frac{C_{1}}{|z|^{2}}\int_{B_{2|z|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z|}{2}}}|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}|^{2}dx\leq\frac{2C_{1}\varepsilon_{0}}{|z|^{2}}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\frac{1}{2}))}\leq 4\sqrt{\pi C_{1}\varepsilon_{0}}.

Now, assume by contradiction that there exists
\varepsilon_{1}>0
, a sequence
\{\vec{\Phi}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C^{\infty}(B(0,R_{k}),M^{m})
of smooth Willmore immersions and a sequence
\left\{z_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in\Omega_{k}(\frac{1}{2})
such that

\displaystyle\log\left|\frac{|z_{k}|}{r_{k}}\right|\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\infty\qquad\text{and}\qquad\log\left|\frac{R_{k}}{|z_{k}|}\right|\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\infty\,,

and

\displaystyle|z_{k}||\nabla\vec{n}_{k}(z_{k})|\geq\varepsilon_{1}>0\,.

(5.1)

Notice that, in particular,
z_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0\in\mathbb{C}
.
Therefore,
applying again the
\varepsilon
-regularity Theorem
4.9
, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2|z_{k}|}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{|z_{k}|}{2}}}|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}|^{2}dx\geq C_{0}^{-1}|z_{k}|^{2}|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}(z_{k})|^{2}\geq C_{0}^{-1}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\,.

(5.2)

Using the previous result (Theorem
3.16
and the end of Section
3.16
)
and that
\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}R_{k}<\infty
, we get that there exist
\alpha_{0}>0
,
\vec{L}_{k}\in\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}_{\lambda_{k}}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}),\mathbb{C}^{n})
,
S_{k}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}),\mathbb{C})
and
\vec{R}_{k}\in\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}),\Lambda^{2}\mathbb{C}^{n})
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla_{z}\vec{L}_{k}&=i\left(\nabla_{z}\vec{H}_{k}-3\,\pi_{\vec{n}_{k}}(\nabla_{z}\vec{H}_{k})-i\star_{h}\left(\nabla_{z}\vec{n}_{k}\wedge\vec{H}_{k}\right)\right)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0})\\
\partial_{z}S_{k}&=\langle\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}_{k},\overline{\vec{L}_{k}}\rangle\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0})\\
\nabla_{z}\vec{R}_{k}&=\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}_{k}\wedge\overline{\vec{L}_{k}}-2i\,\partial_{z}\vec{\Phi}_{k}\wedge\vec{H}_{k}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0})\end{aligned}\right.

(5.3)

and satisfying the bounds:

\displaystyle\left\|e^{\lambda_{k}}\vec{L}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}))}+\left\|\nabla S_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}))}+\left\|\nabla\vec{R}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}))}\leq C

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{L}_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{1,(2,\infty)}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha_{0}))}+\left\|\mathrm{Im}\,(S_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{2,q}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))}+\left\|\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{2,q}(B(0,\alpha_{0}R_{k}))}\leq C_{q},\;\text{ for all $q<\frac{2}{2-\varepsilon}$.}

Furthermore,
\mathrm{Im}\,(S_{k})
and
\mathrm{Im}\,(\vec{R}_{k})
solve the equations
